expand_prior_precision(prior_prec, model):\n    theta = parameters_to_vector(model.parameters())\n    device, P = theta.device, len(theta)\n    assert prior_prec.ndim == 1\n    if len(prior_prec) == 1:  # scalar\n        return torch.ones(P, device=device) * prior_prec\n    elif len(prior_prec) == P:  # full diagonal\n        return prior_prec.to(device)\n    else:\n        return torch.cat([delta * torch.ones_like(m).flatten() for delta, m\n                          in zip(prior_prec, model.parameters())])\n\n\ndef get_prior_hyperparams(prior_prec_init, prior_structure, H, P, device):\n    log_prior_prec_init = np.log(prior_prec_init)\n    if prior_structure == 'scalar':\n        log_prior_prec = log_prior_prec_init * torch.ones(1, device=device)\n    elif prior_structure == 'layerwise':\n        log_prior_prec = log_prior_prec_init * torch.ones(H, device=device)\n    elif prior_structure == 'diagonal':\n        log_prior_prec = log_prior_prec_init * torch.ones(P, device=device)\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(f'Invalid prior structure {prior_structure}')\n    log_prior_prec.requires_grad = True\n    return log_prior_prec\n\n\ndef valid_performance(model, test_loader, likelihood, criterion, method, device):\n    N = len(test_loader.dataset)\n    perf = 0\n    nll = 0\n    for X, y in test_loader:\n        X, y = X.detach().to(device), y.detach().to(device)\n        with torch.no_grad():\n            if method == 'lila':\n                f = model(X).mean(dim=1)\n            else:\n                f = model(X)\n        if likelihood == 'classification':\n            perf += (torch.argmax(f, dim=-1) == y).sum() / N\n        else:\n            perf += (f - y).square().sum() / N\n        nll += criterion(f, y) / len(test_loader)\n    return perf.item(), nll\n\n\ndef get_scheduler(scheduler, optimizer, train_loader, n_epochs, lr, lr_min):\n    n_steps = n_epochs * len(train_loader)\n    if scheduler == 'exp':\n        min_lr_factor = lr_min / lr\n        gamma = np.exp(np.log(min_lr_factor) / n_steps)\n        return ExponentialLR(optimizer, gamma=gamma)\n    elif scheduler == 'cos':\n        return CosineAnnealingLR(optimizer, n_steps, eta_min=lr_min)\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(f'Invalid scheduler {scheduler}')\n\n\ndef get_model_optimizer(optimizer, model, lr, weight_decay=0):\n    if optimizer == 'Adam':\n        return Adam(model.parameters(), lr=lr, weight_decay=weight_decay)\n    elif optimizer == 'SGD':\n        # fixup parameters should have 10x smaller learning rate\n        is_fixup = lambda param: param.size() == torch.Size([1])  # scalars\n        fixup_params = [p for p in model.parameters() if is_fixup(p)]\n        standard_params = [p for p in model.parameters() if not is_fixup(p)]\n        params = [{'params': standard_params}, {'params': fixup_params, 'lr': lr / 10.}]\n        return SGD(params, lr=lr, momentum=0.9, weight_decay=weight_decay)\n    else:\n        raise ValueError(f'Invalid optimizer {optimizer}')\n\n\ndef gradient_to_vector(parameters):\n    return parameters_to_vector([e.grad for e in parameters])\n\n\ndef vector_to_gradient(vec, parameters):\n    return vector_to_parameters(vec, [e.grad for e in parameters])\n\n\ndef marglik_optimization(model,\n                         train_loader,\n                         marglik_loader=None,\n                         valid_loader=None,\n                         partial_loader=None,\n                         likelihood='classification',\n                         prior_structure='layerwise',\n                         prior_prec_init=1.,\n                         sigma_noise_init=1.,\n                         temperature=1.,\n                         n_epochs=500,\n                         lr=1e-1,\n                         lr_min=None,\n                         optimizer='SGD',\n                         scheduler='cos',\n                         n_epochs_burnin=0,\n                         n_hypersteps=100,\n                         n_hypersteps_prior=1,\n                         marglik_frequency=1,\n                         lr_hyp=1e-1,\n                         lr_hyp_min=1e-1,\n                         lr_aug=1e-2,\n                         lr_aug_min=1e-2,\n                         laplace=KronLaplace,\n                         backend=AsdlGGN,\n                         independent=False,\n                         single_output=False,\n                         single_output_iid=False,\n                         kron_jac=True,\n                         method='baseline',\n                         augmenter=None,\n                         stochastic_grad=False,\n                         use_wandb=False):\n    \"\"\"Runs marglik optimization training for a given model and training dataloader.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    model : torch.nn.Module\n        torch model\n    train_loader : DataLoader\n        pytorch training dataset loader\n    marglik_loader : DataLoader\n        pytorch data loader for fitting Laplace\n    valid_loader : DataLoader\n        pytorch data loader for validation\n    partial_loader : DataLoader\n        pytorch data loader for partial fitting for lila's grad accumulation\n    likelihood : str\n        'classification' or 'regression'\n    prior_structure : str\n        'scalar', 'layerwise', 'diagonal'\n    prior_prec_init : float\n        initial prior precision\n    sigma_noise_init : float\n        initial observation noise (for regression only)\n    temperature : float\n        factor for the likelihood for 'overcounting' data.\n        Often required when using data augmentation.\n    n_epochs : int\n    lr : float\n        learning rate for model optimizer\n    lr_min : float\n        minimum learning rate, defaults to lr and hence no decay\n        to have the learning rate decay from 1e-3 to 1e-6, set\n        lr=1e-3 and lr_min=1e-6.\n    optimizer : str\n        either 'Adam' or 'SGD'\n    scheduler : str\n        either 'exp' for exponential and 'cos' for cosine decay towards lr_min\n    n_epochs_burnin : int default=0\n        how many epochs to train without estimating and differentiating marglik\n    n_hypersteps : int\n        how many steps to take on diff. hyperparameters when marglik is estimated\n    n_hypersteps_prior : int\n        how many steps to take on the prior when marglik is estimated\n    marglik_frequency : int\n        how often to estimate (and differentiate) the marginal likelihood\n    lr_hyp : float\n        learning rate for hyperparameters (should be between 1e-3 and 1)\n    lr_hyp_min : float\n        minimum learning rate, decayed to using cosine schedule\n    lr_aug : float\n        learning rate for augmentation parameters\n    lr_aug_min : float\n        minimum learning rate, decayed to using cosine schedule\n    laplace : Laplace\n        type of Laplace approximation (Kron/Diag/Full)\n    backend : Backend\n        AsdlGGN/AsdlEF or BackPackGGN/BackPackEF\n    independent : bool\n        whether to use independent functional laplace\n    single_output : bool\n        whether to use single random output for functional laplace\n    single_output_iid : bool\n        whether to sample single output per sample iid (otherwise per batch)\n    kron_jac : bool\n        whether to use kron_jac in the backend\n    method : augmentation strategy, one of ['baseline'] -> no change\n        or ['lila'] -> change in protocol.\n    augmenter : torch.nn.Module with differentiable parameter\n    stochastic_grad : bool\n        whether to use stochastic gradients of marginal likelihood\n        usually would correspond to lower bound (unless small data)\n\n    Returns\n    -------\n    lap : Laplace\n        lapalce approximation\n    model : torch.nn.Module\n    margliks : list\n    valid_perfs : list\n    aug_history: list\n        None for method == 'baseline'\n    \"\"\"\n    if lr_min is None:  # don't decay lr\n        lr_min = lr\n    if marglik_loader is None:\n        marglik_loader = train_loader\n    if partial_loader is None:\n        partial_loader = marglik_loader\n    device = parameters_to_vector(model.parameters()).device\n    N = len(train_loader.dataset)\n    H = len(list(model.parameters()))\n    P = len(parameters_to_vector(model.parameters()))\n    optimize_aug = augmenter is not None and parameters_to_vector(augmenter.parameters()).requires_grad\n    backend_kwargs = dict(differentiable=(stochastic_grad and optimize_aug) or laplace is FunctionalLaplace,\n                          kron_jac=kron_jac)\n    la_kwargs = dict(sod=stochastic_grad, single_output=single_output, single_output_iid=single_output_iid)\n    if laplace is FunctionalLaplace:\n        la_kwargs['independent'] = independent\n    if use_wandb:\n get_prior_hyperparams(prior_prec_init, prior_structure, H, P, device)\n    hyperparameters.append(log_prior_prec)\n\n    # set up loss (and observation noise hyperparam)\n    if likelihood == 'classification':\n        criterion = CrossEntropyLoss(reduction='mean')\n        sigma_noise = 1\n    elif likelihood == 'regression':\n        criterion = MSELoss(reduction='mean')\n        log_sigma_noise_init = np.log(sigma_noise_init)\n        log_sigma_noise = log_sigma_noise_init * torch.ones(1, device=device)\n        log_sigma_noise.requires_grad = True\n        hyperparameters.append(log_sigma_noise)\n\n    # set up model optimizer and scheduler\n    optimizer = get_model_optimizer(optimizer, model, lr)\n    scheduler = get_scheduler(scheduler, optimizer, hyper_scheduler = CosineAnnealingLR(hyper_optimizer, n_steps, eta_min=lr_hyp_min)\n    if optimize_aug:\n        logging.info('MARGLIK: optimize augmentation.')\n        aug_optimizer = Adam(augmenter.parameters(), lr=lr_aug)\n        n_steps = ((n_epochs - n_epochs_burnin) // marglik_frequency) * (n_hypersteps if stochastic_grad else 1)\n        aug_scheduler = CosineAnnealingLR(aug_optimizer, n_steps, eta_min=lr_aug_min)\n        aug_history = [parameters_to_vector(augmenter.parameters()).squeeze().detach().cpu().numpy()]\n\n    losses = list()\n    valid_perfs = list()\n    margliks = list()\n\n    for epoch in range(1, n_epochs + 1):\n        epoch_time_fwd = 0.0\n        epoch_time_fit = 0.0\n\n        epoch_loss = 0\n        epoch_perf = 0\n        epoch_nll = 0\n        epoch_log = dict(epoch=epoch)\n\n        # standard NN training per batch\n        torch.cuda.empty_cache()\n        for X, y in train_loader:\n            X, y = X.detach().to(device), y.to(device)\n            optimizer.zero_grad()\n\n            if likelihood == 'regression':\n                sigma_noise = torch.exp(log_sigma_noise).detach()\n                crit_factor = 1 / temperature / (2 * sigma_noise.square())\n            else:\n                crit_factor = 1 / temperature\n            prior_prec = torch.exp(log_prior_prec).detach()\n            delta = expand_prior_precision(prior_prec, model)\n\n            # fit data\n            time_fwd = time()\n            if method == 'lila':\n                f = model(X).mean(dim=1)\n            else:\n                f = model(X)\n            epoch_time_fwd += time() - time_fwd # log total time fwd fit in epoch\n\n            time_fit = time()\n            theta = parameters_to_vector(model.parameters())\n            loss = criterion(f, y) + (0.5 * (delta * theta) @ theta) / N / crit_factor\n            loss.backward()\n            optimizer.step()\n            epoch_time_fit += time() - time_fit # log total time bwd fit in epoch\n\n            epoch_loss += loss.cpu().item() / len(train_loader)\n            epoch_nll += criterion(f.detach(), y).item() / len(train_loader)\n            if likelihood == 'regression':\n                epoch_perf += (f.detach() - y).square().sum() / N\n            else:\n                epoch_perf += torch.sum(torch.argmax(f.detach(), dim=-1) == y).item() / N\n            scheduler.step()\n\n        losses.append(epoch_loss)\n        logging.info('MAP memory allocated: ' + str(torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated(loss.device)/GB_FACTOR) + ' Gb.')\n        logging.info(f'MARGLIK[epoch={epoch}]: train. perf={epoch_perf*100:.2f}%; loss={epoch_loss:.5f}; nll={epoch_nll:.5f}')\n        optimizer.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)\n        llr = scheduler.get_last_lr()[0]\n        epoch_log.update({'train/loss': epoch_loss, 'train/nll': epoch_nll, 'train/perf': epoch_perf, 'train/lr': llr,\n                          'train/time_fwd': epoch_time_fwd, 'train/time_fit': epoch_time_fit})\n        if use_wandb and ((epoch % 5) == 0):\n            wandb_log_parameter_norm(model)\n\n        # compute validation error to report during training\n        if valid_loader is not None:\n            with torch.no_grad():\n                val_perf, val_nll = valid_performance(model, valid_loader, likelihood, criterion, method, device)\n                valid_perfs.append(val_perf)\n                logging.info(f'MARGLIK[epoch={epoch}]: valid. perf={val_perf*100:.2f}%; nll={val_nll:.5f}.')\n                epoch_log.update({'valid/perf': val_perf, 'valid/nll': val_nll})\n\n        # only update hyperparameters every \"Frequency\" steps after \"burnin\"\n        if (epoch % marglik_frequency) != 0 or epoch < n_epochs_burnin:\n            if use_wandb:\n                wandb.log(epoch_log, step=epoch, commit=((epoch % 10) == 0))\n            continue\n\n        # optimizer hyperparameters by differentiating marglik\n        time_hyper = time()\n\n        # 1. fit laplace approximation\n        torch.cuda.empty_cache()\n        if optimize_aug:\n            if stochastic_grad:  # differentiable\n                marglik_loader.attach()\n            else:  # jvp\n                marglik_loader.detach()\n\n        # first optimize prior precision jointly with direct marglik grad\n        margliks_local = list()\n        n_hyper = max(n_hypersteps_prior, n_hypersteps) if stochastic_grad else n_hypersteps_prior\n        for i in range(n_hyper):\n            if i == 0 or stochastic_grad:\n                sigma_noise = 1 if likelihood == 'classification' else torch.exp(log_sigma_noise)\n                prior_prec = torch.exp(log_prior_prec)\n                lap = laplace(model, likelihood, sigma_noise=sigma_noise, prior_precision=prior_prec,\n                              temperature=temperature, backend=backend, backend_kwargs=backend_kwargs,\n                              **la_kwargs)\n                lap.fit(marglik_loader)\n            if i < n_hypersteps and optimize_aug and stochastic_grad:\n                aug_optimizer.zero_grad()\n            if i < n_hypersteps_prior:\n                hyper_optimizer.zero_grad()\n            if i < n_hypersteps_prior and not stochastic_grad:  # does not fit every it\n                sigma_noise = None if likelihood == 'classification' else torch.exp(log_sigma_noise)\n                prior_prec = torch.exp(log_prior_prec)\n                marglik = -lap.log_marginal_likelihood(prior_prec, sigma_noise) / N\n            else:  # fit with updated hparams\n                marglik = -lap.log_marginal_likelihood() / N\n            marglik.backward()\n            margliks_local.append(marglik.item())\n            if i < n_hypersteps_prior:\n                hyper_optimizer.step()\n                hyper_scheduler.step()\n            if i < n_hypersteps and optimize_aug and stochastic_grad:\n                aug_optimizer.step()\n                aug_scheduler.step()\n\n        if stochastic_grad:\n            marglik = np.mean(margliks_local)\n        else:\n            marglik = margliks_local[-1]\n\n        if use_wandb:\n            wandb_log_prior(torch.exp(log_prior_prec.detach()), prior_structure, model)\n        if likelihood == 'regression':\n            epoch_log['hyperparams/sigma_noise'] = torch.exp(log_sigma_noise.detach()).cpu().item()\n        epoch_log['train/marglik'] = marglik\n        logging.info('LA memory allocated: ' + str(torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated(loss.device)/GB_FACTOR) + ' Gb.')\n\n        # option 2: jvp (not direct_grad)\n        torch.cuda.empty_cache()\n        if optimize_aug and not stochastic_grad:  # accumulate gradient with JVP\n            partial_loader.attach()\n            aug_grad = torch.zeros_like(parameters_to_vector(augmenter.parameters()))\n            lap.backend.differentiable = True\n            if isinstance(lap, KronLaplace):\n                # does the inversion internally\n                hess_inv = lap.posterior_precision.jvp_logdet()\n            else:\n                hess_inv = lap.posterior_covariance.flatten()\n            for i, (X, y) in zip(range(n_hypersteps), partial_loader):\n                lap.loss, H_batch = lap._curv_closure(X, y, N)\n                # curv closure creates gradient already, need to zero\n                aug_optimizer.zero_grad()\n                # compute grad wrt. neg. log-lik\n                (- lap.log_likelihood).backward(inputs=list(augmenter.parameters()), retain_graph=True)\n                # compute grad wrt. log det = 0.5 vec(P_inv) @ (grad-vec H)\n                (0.5 * H_batch.flatten()).backward(gradient=hess_inv, inputs=list(augmenter.parameters()))\n                aug_grad = (aug_grad + gradient_to_vector(augmenter.parameters()).data.clone())\n\n            lap.backend.differentiable = False\n\n            vector_to_gradient(aug_grad, augmenter.parameters())\n            aug_optimizer.step()\n            aug_scheduler.step()\n\n        epoch_time_hyper = time() - time_hyper\n        epoch_log.update({'train/time_hyper': epoch_time_hyper})\n\n        if optimize_aug:\n            aug_history.append(parameters_to_vector(augmenter.parameters()).squeeze().detach().cpu().numpy())\n            logging.info(f'Augmentation params epoch {epoch}: {aug_history[-1]}')\n            if margliks.append(marglik)\n        del lap\n        if use_wandb:\n            if optimize_aug:\n                epoch_log['train/lr_aug'] = aug_scheduler.get_last_lr()[0]\n logging.info(f'MARGLIK[epoch={epoch}]: marglik optimization. MargLik={margliks[-1]:.5f}, prec: {prior_prec.detach().mean().item():.2f}.')\n\n    sigma_noise = 1 if sigma_noise is None else sigma_noise\n    lap = laplace(model, likelihood, sigma_noise=sigma_noise, prior_precision=prior_prec,\n                  temperature=temperature, backend=backend, backend_kwargs=backend_kwargs,\n                  **la_kwargs)\n    lap.fit(marglik_loader.detach())\n    if optimize_aug:\n        return lap, model, margliks, valid_perfs, aug_history\n    return lap, model, margliks, valid_perfs, None",
    "Experiment Result": "Common experimental settings across `bound_grid.py` and `classification_image.py` for methods and bounds:\n- `device`: 'cuda'\n- `seed`: Integer (e.g., 1, 2, 3, 7, 711)\n- `method`: 'baseline' (no augmentation) or 'lila' (invariance learning with augmentation)\n- `approx`: Laplace approximation type, choices are ['full', 'kron', 'blockdiag', 'diag', 'kernel']\n- `curv`: Curvature type, choices are ['ggn', 'ef']\n- `dataset`: Varied datasets including 'mnist', 'mnist_r180', 'fmnist', 'cifar10', 'cifar100', 'tinyimagenet' (some with rotations, translations, or scaling)\n- `model`: Network architecture, choices are ['mininet', 'mlp', 'cnn', 'resnet', 'wrn']\n- `n_epochs`: Number of training epochs (e.g., 100, 300, 500)\n- `n_epochs_burnin`: Number of epochs to train without marginal likelihood optimization (e.g., 10, 1000)\n- `batch_size`: Mini-batch size for training (e.g., 128, 250, 500, or subset_size if <=0)\n- `marglik_batch_size`: Batch size for fitting Laplace approximation (e.g., 100, 1000, or subset_size if <=0)\n- `partial_batch_size`: Batch size for JVP computations (e.g., 60, or subset_size if <=0)\n- `subset_size`: Number of observations in generated data (e.g., 1000, or -1 for full dataset)\n- `bound`: Boolean flag, `True` to use lower bound estimators (implies `stochastic_grad = True` and uses `SubsetTensorDataLoader` or `GroupedSubsetTensorDataLoader`), `False` to aggregate full batch\n- `n_hypersteps`: Number of steps on hyperparameters per marginal likelihood estimate (e.g., 1, 2, 100)\n- `n_hypersteps_prior`: Number of steps for prior precision (e.g., 1, 2, 10)\n- `marglik_frequency`: How often to estimate marginal likelihood (e.g., 1, 5)\n- `n_samples_aug`: Number of augmentation samples for 'lila' (e.g., 30, 31)\n- `random_flip`: Boolean, randomly flip input images (only for 'lila')\n- `prior_prec_init`: Initial prior precision (e.g., 1.0)\n- `lr`: Learning rate for model optimizer (e.g., 1e-1, 1e-3, 0.1)\n- `lr_min`: Minimum learning rate for model optimizer (e.g., 1e-4, 1e-8, 0.1)\n- `lr_hyp`: Learning rate for hyperparameters (e.g., 1e-1, 0.1)\n- `lr_hyp_min`: Minimum learning rate for hyperparameters (e.g., 1e-2, 0.1)\n- `lr_aug`: Learning rate for augmentation parameters (e.g., 0.005, 0.05)\n- `lr_aug_min`: Minimum learning rate for augmentation parameters (e.g., 0.00001, 0.005)\n- `temperature`: Likelihood temperature (e.g., 1.0)\n- `independent_outputs`: Boolean, whether to use independent outputs for FunctionalLaplace (used with `single_output`)\n- `single_output`: Boolean, whether to sample only a single output (output-wise partitioning)\n- `single_output_iid`: Boolean, only applies when `single_output=True`, samples single output iid instead of one output per batch.\n- `kron_jac`: Boolean, whether to use Kronecker approximation for Jacobians\n- `grouped_loader`: Boolean, whether to use `GroupedSubsetTensorDataLoader` for subset sampling (class-wise partitioning)\n- `stochastic_grad`: Boolean, whether to use stochastic gradients of marginal likelihood (derived from `bound` flag)\n\nSpecific settings in `bound_grid.py`:\n- Initial training uses `laplace=FunctionalLaplace` to optimize `prior_precision` or `augmenter.rot_factor`.\n- Assessment of bounds is done on a grid:\n  - `batch_sizes`: `[10, 20, 50, 100, 250, 500, 1000]` when `stochastic=True`, `[1000]` otherwise.\n  - `hparam grid`: `np.logspace(-4, 4, 100)` for 'prior' setting (prior precision), `np.linspace(0, np.pi, 100)` for 'invariance' setting (rotation factor).\n  - `n_reps`: `int(subset_size / batch_size)` repetitions for each grid point.\n\nSpecific settings in `classification_image.py`:\n- Default optimizer for `mininet` model is `Adam`, otherwise `SGD`.\n- `prior_structure` defaults to `layerwise`.\n- `data_root`: 'data' or '/is/cluster/fast/aimmer/data' for specific datasets.\n- Logging with `wandb` is enabled."
}{
    "Title": "PriorBand: Practical Hyperparameter Optimization in the Age of Deep Learning",
    "Main Contributions": "PriorBand addresses the misalignment between existing Hyperparameter Optimization (HPO) algorithms and modern Deep Learning (DL) practice, which often relies on costly manual experimentation. The main contributions are: 1. Developing the first approach to leverage cheap proxy tasks alongside expert prior input for HPO. 2. Proposing PriorBand, a general HPO algorithm that fulfills all six identified desiderata for DL HPO (strong performance under low budgets, integration of cheap proxy tasks, integration of expert beliefs, handling mixed search spaces, simplicity, and parallelism). 3. Demonstrating PriorBand's efficiency and robustness on a wide suite of DL tasks within practical compute resources. 4. Highlighting PriorBand's flexible, modular design by improving various multi-fidelity algorithms and their model-based extensions.",
    "Methodology": "PriorBand extends HyperBand (HB) by replacing its random sampling component with an Ensemble Sampling Policy (ESP) called Eπ. Eπ combines three sampling strategies: uniform random sampling (pU), prior-based sampling (pπ), and incumbent-based sampling (pˆλ). The proportion of random sampling (pU) geometrically decays with increasing fidelity (rung), while prior-based sampling (pπ) geometrically increases. Incumbent-based sampling is activated after the first SH bracket and at least one configuration is evaluated at maximum fidelity. It perturbs the current best configuration by selecting hyperparameters with a probability (fixed at 0.5) and applying Gaussian noise (σ=0.25) for continuous HPs, or a weighted discrete distribution for categorical HPs. The trade-off between prior-based and incumbent-based sampling is dynamically determined by computing weighted scores (Sπ, Sˆλ) based on the likelihood of top-performing configurations under both the expert prior and the incumbent's local distribution, normalizing these scores to set pπ and pˆλ. PriorBand's objective is to minimize an expensive objective function using a cheap multi-fidelity proxy, guided by a user-specified belief about the optimal configuration.",
    "Experimental Setup": "The experimental setup involved 12 curated benchmarks covering diverse search spaces (mixed-type, log-scaled HPs) and downstream tasks, including 4 PD1 benchmarks (large transformers), 5 LCBench benchmarks, and 3 JAHSBench benchmarks (architecture and training HPs). Multi-fidelity synthetic Hartmann functions were also used. Baselines included HyperBand (HB), ASHA, Asynchronous-HB, vanilla Bayesian Optimization (BO), BOHB, πBO, and Mobster, along with custom ESP-augmented variants. Expert priors were generated in three qualities: 'near optimum' (best of 50k samples with perturbation), 'good' (best of 25 random samples), and 'bad' (worst of 50k samples). All priors were Gaussian N(λ, σ^2) with σ=0.25. Experiments were conducted for single-worker (50 seeds, 12 benchmarks) and multi-worker (4 workers, 10 seeds, 7 benchmarks due to LCBench issues) scenarios. Budgets ranged from 5 to 20 full trainings. Performance was measured by average relative ranks and normalized regret of the incumbent configuration (lowest validation error) at maximum fidelity. The official BOHB implementation was used, while others were re-implemented, with code open-sourced.",
    "Limitations": "PriorBand inherits known pathologies of HyperBand, such as sensitivity to poor fidelity correlations, choice of fidelity bounds, and early stopping rates. While supporting any prior distribution, experiments primarily used a fixed Gaussian distribution with a standard deviation of 0.25. PriorBand is not entirely free of hyperparameters (e.g., perturbation probability p=0.5, continuous HP perturbation σ=0.25), which were fixed in experiments to reduce confounding factors. The experimental compute budgets (approximately 10-12 full trainings) might not be universally feasible for all deep learning experts. However, the study emphasizes PriorBand's strong anytime performance under even shorter HPO budgets, especially when informative priors are available.",
    "Future Research Directions": "Future research could explore dynamically adjusting PriorBand's hyperparameters for local search, such as implementing a schedule to reduce the standard deviation of Gaussian perturbation noise or adaptively changing the probability for hyperparameter perturbation as optimization progresses, potentially leading to better exploitation.     "Experiment Code": "The core implementation details of PriorBand's Ensemble Sampling Policy (Eπ), including its dynamic proportions and specific perturbation mechanisms for incumbent-based sampling, are not directly present in the provided repository content as a standalone optimizer class. However, the following sections represent its operational context and closely related functionalities:\n\n1.  **Orchestration of PriorBand Searcher (from `src/mf_prior_experiments/run.py`):** This function shows how an optimizer (which PriorBand is) is instantiated and integrated into the NePS framework, handling benchmark queries and fidelity management. It represents the interface through which the PriorBand algorithm is run within experiments.\n```python\ndef run_neps(args):\n    # NOTE THIS HERE: this bypasses this function and runs the gptbench version\n    if \"charLM\" in args.benchmark.name:\n        run_neps_on_gptbench(args)\n        return\n\n    from mfpbench import Benchmark\n\n    import neps\n\n    # Added the type here just for editors to be able to get a quick view\n    benchmark: Benchmark = hydra.utils.instantiate(args.benchmark.api)  # type: ignore\n\n    def run_pipeline(previous_pipeline_directory: Path, **config: Any) -> dict:\n        start = time.time()\n        if benchmark.fidelity_name in config:\n            fidelity = config.pop(benchmark.fidelity_name)\n        else:\n            fidelity = benchmark.fidelity_range[1]\n\n        result = benchmark.query(config, at=fidelity)\n\n        # This design only makes sense in the context of surrogate/tabular\n        # benchmarks, where we do not actually need to run the model being\n        # queried.\n        max_fidelity_result = benchmark.query(config, at=benchmark.end)\n\n        # To account for continuations of previous configs in the parallel setting,\n        # we use the `previous_pipeline_directory` which indicates if there has been\n        # a previous lower fidelity evaluation of this config. If that's the case we\n        # then subtract the previous fidelity off of this current one to compute\n        # the `continuation_fidelity`. Otherwise, the `continuation_fidelity` is\n        # just the current one. This is then used to make the worker sleep and\n        # so we get a hueristically correct setup for each worker. In contrast,\n        # if we do not do this, workers will not have even close to the correct\n        # timestamps, and the order in which workers pick up new configurations to\n        # evaluate may be in a very different order than if done in a real context.\n        if args.n_workers == 1:\n            # In the single worker setting, this does not matter and we can use\n            # post-processing of the results to calculate the `continuation_fidelity`.\n            continuation_fidelity = None\n        else:\n            # If there's no previous config, we sleep for `fidelity`.\n            if previous_pipeline_directory is None:\n                continuation_fidelity = None\n                fidelity_sleep_time = fidelity\n\n            # If there is a previous config, we calculate the `continuation_fidelity`\n            # and sleep for that time instead\n            else:\n                previous_results_file = previous_pipeline_directory / \"result.yaml\"\n                with previous_results_file.open(\"r\") as f:\n                    previous_results = yaml.load(f, Loader=yaml.FullLoader)\n\n                # Calculate the continuation fidelity for sleeping\n                current_fidelity = fidelity\n                previous_fidelity = previous_results[\"info_dict\"][\"fidelity\"]\n                continuation_fidelity = current_fidelity - previous_fidelity\n\n                logger.info(\"-\"*30)\n                logger.info(f\"Continuing from: {previous_pipeline_directory}\")\n                logger.info(f\"`continuation_fidelity`={continuation_fidelity}`\")\n                logger.info(f\"{previous_results}\")\n                logger.info(\"-\"*30)\n\n\n                fidelity_sleep_time = continuation_fidelity\n\n            time.sleep(fidelity_sleep_time + MIN_SLEEP_TIME)\n\n        end = time.time()\n        return {\n            \"loss\": result.error,\n            \"cost\": result.cost,\n            \"info_dict\": {\n                \"cost\": result.cost,\n                \"val_score\": result.val_score,\n                \"test_score\": result.test_score,\n                \"fidelity\": result.fidelity,\n                \"continuation_fidelity\": continuation_fidelity,\n                \"start_time\": start,\n                \"end_time\": end,  # + fidelity,\n                \"max_fidelity_loss\": float(max_fidelity_result.error),\n                \"max_fidelity_cost\": float(max_fidelity_result.cost),\n                \"process_id\": os.getpid(),\n                # val_error: result.val_error\n                # test_error: result.test_error\n            },\n        }\n\n    lower, upper, _ = benchmark.fidelity_range\n    fidelity_name = benchmark.fidelity_name\n\n    pipeline_space = {\"search_space\": benchmark.space}\n    if args.algorithm.mf:\n        if isinstance(lower, float):\n            fidelity_param = neps.FloatParameter(\n                lower=lower, upper=upper, is_fidelity=True\n            )\n        else:\n            fidelity_param = neps.IntegerParameter(\n                lower=lower, upper=upper, is_fidelity=True\n            )\n        pipeline_space = {**pipeline_space, **{fidelity_name: fidelity_param}}\n        logger.info(f\"Using fidelity space: \\n {fidelity_param}\")\n    # pipeline_space = {\"search_space\": benchmark.space, fidelity_name: fidelity_param}\n    logger.info(f\"Using search space: \\n {pipeline_space}\")\n\n    # TODO: could we pass budget per benchmark\n    # TODO: figure out how to pass runtime budget and if metahyper internally\n        #  calculates continuation costs to subtract from optimization budget\n        # **budget_args,\n        max_evaluations_total=max_evaluations_total,\n        searcher=hydra.utils.instantiate(args.algorithm.searcher, _partial_=True),\n        overwrite_working_directory=OVERWRITE,\n    )\n```\n\n2.  **Prior Enhancement Mechanism (from `src/mf_prior_algorithms/random_search.py`):** While belonging to a baseline `RandomSearchWithPriors` optimizer, the `_enhance_priors` method demonstrates a mechanism to apply confidence scores to hyperparameters based on their type (Float/Integer or Categorical). This is directly relevant to how PriorBand's prior-based sampling (`pπ`) would weight different hyperparameters, potentially influencing Gaussian noise standard deviation for continuous HPs and discrete distributions for categorical HPs.\n```python\nclass RandomSearchWithPriors(RandomSearch):\n    use_priors = True\n\n    def __init__(self, prior_confidence: str = \"medium\", **optimizer_kwargs):\n        super().__init__(**optimizer_kwargs)\n        self.prior_confidence = prior_confidence\n        self._enhance_priors()\n\n    def _enhance_priors(self, confidence_score=None):\n        \"\"\"Only applicable when priors are given along with a confidence.\n\n        Args:\n            confidence_score: dict\n                The confidence scores for the types.\n                Example: {\"categorical\": 5.2, \"numeric\": 0.15}\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self.use_priors and self.prior_confidence is None:\n            return\n        for k, v in self.pipeline_space.items():\n            if v.is_fidelity or isinstance(v, ConstantParameter):\n                continue\n            elif isinstance(v, (FloatParameter, IntegerParameter)):\n                if confidence_score is None:\n                    confidence = CUSTOM_FLOAT_CONFIDENCE_SCORES[self.prior_confidence]\n                else:\n                    confidence = confidence_score[\"numeric\"]\n                self.pipeline_space[k].default_confidence_score = confidence\n            elif isinstance(v, CategoricalParameter):\n                if confidence_score is None:\n                    confidence = CUSTOM_CATEGORICAL_CONFIDENCE_SCORES[\n                        self.prior_confidence\n                    ]\n                else:\n                    confidence = confidence_score[\"categorical\"]\n                self.pipeline_space[k].default_confidence_score = confidence\n```",
    "Experiment Result": "The experimental settings for methods like PriorBand are configured through various files:\n\n1.  **Prior Definition and Perturbation Settings (`src/mf_prior_experiments/configs/benchmark/generate.py`):**\n    -   `PRIORS_TO_DO = [\"medium\", \"good\", \"at25\", \"bad\"]`: Specifies the four types of priors used across different benchmarks.\n    -   `MEDIUM_PERTURB_STRENGTH = 0.25`: Defines the perturbation strength applied when a \"medium\" prior is used.\n    -   For \"good\" priors, `api.update({\"noisy_prior\": True, \"prior_noise_scale\": 0.250 })` is applied. This means a good prior might also have an associated noise scale for its application.\n    -   For \"medium\" priors, `api.update({\"perturb_prior\": MEDIUM_PERTURB_STRENGTH})` is used, linking the medium prior directly to the defined perturbation strength.\n    -   `CONFIGSPACE_SEED = 133_077` is used for benchmark sampling and querying to determine `best_N_error` values, which serve as baselines for comparison.\n\n2.  **Experiment Execution Settings (`src/mf_prior_experiments/run.py`):**\n    -   `max_evaluations_total`: Set to `130` for multi-fidelity (MF) algorithms and `25` for single-fidelity algorithms. This defines the total number of evaluations allowed.\n    -   `MIN_SLEEP_TIME = 10` (seconds): Used in the `run_pipeline` function for parallel workers to simulate waiting times and account for `continuation_fidelity` when calculating wall-clock time, influencing the perceived cost of multi-fidelity evaluations.\n\n3.  **Algorithm Naming and Plotting Styles (`src/mf_prior_experiments/plot_styles.py`):**\n    -   The `ALGORITHMS` dictionary provides human-readable names for various algorithms, including different configurations of PriorBand. For example, `\"pb_mutation_dynamic_geometric-default-at-target\"` is mapped to `\"PriorBand\"`, `\"pb_mutation_decay_geometric-default-at-target\"` to `\"PriorBand(decay)\"`, and `\"pb_mutation_dynamic_geometric_bo-default-at-target\"` to `\"PriorBand+BO\"`. These mappings indicate the naming conventions used in results presentation.\n    -   `Y_LABEL = \"Error\"` and `X_LABEL = {\"cumulated_fidelity\": \"Full trainings\"}` define the default labels for the y-axis (objective function) and x-axis (cumulative budget/fidelity) in plots."

    "Title": "Federated Hyperparameter Tuning: Challenges, Baselines, and Connections to Weight-Sharing",
    "Main Contributions": "The paper systematically analyzes the problem of federated hyperparameter tuning, identifying key challenges such as federated validation data, extreme resource limitations, and costly personalization evaluation. It introduces a novel method called FedEx, which accelerates federated hyperparameter tuning by making a new connection to the weight-sharing technique from neural architecture search (NAS). FedEx is applicable to widely-used federated optimization methods like FedAvg. The work provides theoretical guarantees for a FedEx variant to correctly tune the on-device learning rate in online convex optimization across devices, showing improved convergence rates. Empirically, FedEx outperforms natural baselines by several percentage points on Shakespeare, FEMNIST, and CIFAR-10 benchmarks, achieving higher accuracy within the same training budget. This is presented as the first systematic study of hyperparameter optimization in the federated setting and the first application of weight-sharing to non-architectural (regular) hyperparameters.",
    "Methodology": "The methodology involves formalizing federated hyperparameter optimization for both global and personalized FL objectives. Baselines are established by adapting standard non-federated algorithms like Random Search (RS) and Successive Halving (SHA) for noisy validation signals. The core contribution is FedEx, which treats local training hyperparameters as trainable model parameters via a weight-sharing approach. It transforms the personalized objective into a single-level empirical risk minimization (ERM) problem and uses a stochastic relaxation where hyperparameters are sampled from a parameterized distribution Dθ. Model weights (w) are updated using standard SGD, while the distribution parameters (θ) are updated using exponentiated gradient, alternating between these two steps. FedEx can be wrapped by RS or SHA to tune server-side hyperparameters and FedEx's own meta-hyperparameters. A local perturbation scheme is used to initialize the discrete configurations for the categorical distribution over hyperparameters. Theoretically, the paper analyzes a FedEx variant using the ARUBA framework in an online convex optimization (OCO) setting to prove guarantees for tuning the client step-size based on task-averaged regret.",
    "Experimental Setup": "Experiments were conducted on three standard federated learning benchmarks: Shakespeare (next-character prediction), FEMNIST (image classification), and CIFAR-10 (image classification). Two data partitioning schemes were used: i.i.d. (randomly shuffled local data) and non-i.i.d. (Shakespeare: actor-based temporal split; FEMNIST: digit writer-based random split; CIFAR-10 only i.i.d.). Data splits for Shakespeare/FEMNIST were 80% training, 10% validation, 10% testing; for CIFAR-10, 10K examples were held out for validation. Backbone models for Shakespeare and CIFAR-10 followed FedAvg evaluation, while FEMNIST used LEAF models. The total communication rounds were 4K for Shakespeare/CIFAR-10 and 2K for FEMNIST. FedEx was instantiated to tune hyperparameters of FedAvg, FedProx, and Reptile. A wide range of server (e.g., learning rate schedule, momentum) and local training (e.g., learning rate, momentum, weight-decay, epochs, batch-size, dropout, proximal regularization) hyperparameters were considered. Performance was evaluated using final test error and online evaluation (test error over communication rounds). Baselines and FedEx were wrapped with RS or SHA (elimination rate η=3). Ablation studies were performed on the local perturbation factor (ε) and step-size schedules for the exponentiated gradient updates. Confidence intervals were derived from 5 (Shakespeare), 10 (FEMNIST), 10 (RS on CIFAR), and 6 (SHA on CIFAR) independent trials.",
    "Limitations": "FedEx is primarily applicable to FL algorithms that can be decomposed into local fine-tuning and aggregation; algorithms without this structure must rely on standard baselines. It requires a wrapper algorithm (e.g., SHA or RS) to tune its own internal hyperparameters (like the baseline λt) and certain server-side FL hyperparameters (e.g., server learning rate). While a local perturbation scheme helps, it still limits the immediate search space of each FedEx instance compared to exploring the entire hyperparameter space at once. The theoretical analysis for step-size tuning is performed in a simplified online setting with specific assumptions (e.g., one client per round, oblivious within-task adversary, zero baseline λt). The theoretical bounds, while improving dependence on τ, introduce an additional m^(1/3) factor. Empirically, FedEx may exhibit slightly less stable performance at the very beginning of training due to its stochastic nature. The paper acknowledges that it does not address the time-dependency of federated evaluation or the inherent privacy and fairness risks associated with federated learning using sensitive data.",
    "Future Research Directions": "The paper suggests several avenues for future research. These include extending FedEx to tune actual architectural hyperparameters, effectively enabling federated Neural Architecture Search (NAS). Another direction is to apply FedEx to tune initialization-based meta-learning algorithms beyond Reptile, such as MAML. The authors also highlight the need to integrate their work with existing tools and research on mitigating privacy and fairness risks in federated learning, given its frequent use of sensitive data. Further investigation into improving the utilization of validation signals beyond simple state-estimation methods is also suggested, as current approaches did not yield significant improvements. Finally, the unaddressed challenge of time-dependency in federated evaluation offers another potential area for future work.",

self.clf(self.dropout(x))def get_prox(model, criterion=nn.CrossEntropyLoss(), mu=0.0):    if not mu:        return criterion    mu *= 0.5    model0 = [param.data.clone() for param in numpy.linalg import normfrom scipy.special import logsumexpfrom tensorboardX import SummaryWriterfrom torch import optimdef discounted_mean(trace, factor=1.0):    weight = factor ** np.flip(np.arange(len(trace)), axis=0)    return np.inner(trace, weight) / weight.sum()class FedEx:    '''runs hyperparameter optimization given a federated learning server'''    def entropy(self):        entropy = 0.0        for probs in product(*(theta[theta>0.0] for theta in self._theta)):            prob = np.prod(probs)            entropy -= prob * np.log(prob)        return entropy    def mle(self):            return np.prod([theta.max() for theta in self._theta])    def __init__(                 self,                  server,                  configs,                  eta0='auto',                  sched='auto',                  cutoff=0.0,                  baseline=0.0,                  diff=False,                 ):        '''        Args:            server: Object that implements two methods, 'communication_round' and 'full_evaluation'                    taking as input a single argument, 'get_config', itself a function that takes                     no inputs and outputs an element of the provided list 'configs'.                     - 'communication_round' samples a batch of clients, assigns a config to each                     using 'get_config', and runs local training using that config. It then                     aggregates the local models to to take a training step and returns three lists                     or arrays: a list of each client's validation error before local training, a                     list of each client's validation error after local training, and a list of each                     client's weight (e.g. size of its validation set).                     - 'full_evaluation' assigns a config to each client using 'get_config' and runs                    local training using that config. It then returns three lists or arrays: a list                    of each client's test error before local training, a list of each client's test                    error after local training, and a list of each client's weight (e.g. size of                     its test set).            configs: list of configs used for local training and testing by 'server'                      OR dict of (string, list) pairs denoting a grid of configs            eta0: base exponentiated gradient step size; if 'auto' uses sqrt(2*log(len(configs)))            sched: learning rate schedule for exponentiated gradient:                    - 'adaptive': uses eta0 / sqrt(sum of squared gradient l-infinity norms)                    - 'aggressive': uses eta0 / gradient l-infinity norm                    - 'auto': uses eta0 / sqrt(t) for t the number of rounds                    - 'constant': uses eta0                    - 'scale': uses sched * sqrt(2 * log(len(configs)))            cutoff: entropy level below which to stop updating the config probability and use MLE            baseline: discount factor when computing baseline; 0.0 is most recent, 1.0 is mean            diff: if True uses performance difference; otherwise uses absolute performance        '''        self._server = server        self._configs = configs        self._grid = [] if type(configs) == list else sorted(configs.keys())        sizes = [len(configs[param]) for param in self._grid] if self._grid else [len(configs)]        self._eta0 = [np.sqrt(2.0 * np.log(size)) if eta0 == 'auto' else eta0 for size in sizes]        self._sched = sched        self._cutoff = cutoff        self._baseline = baseline        self._diff = diff        self._z = [np.full(size, -np.log(size)) for size in sizes]        self._theta = [np.exp(z) for z in self._z]        self._store = [0.0 for _ in sizes]        self._stopped = False        self._trace = {'global': [], 'refine': [], 'entropy': [self.entropy()], 'mle': [self.mle()]}    def stop(self):        self._stopped = True    def sample(self, mle=False, _index=[]):        '''samples from configs using current probability vector'''        if mle or self._stopped:            if self._grid:                return {param: self._configs[param][theta.argmax()]                         for theta, param in zip(self._theta, self._grid)}            return self._configs[self._theta[0].argmax()]        _index.append([np.random.choice(len(theta), p=theta) for theta in self._theta])        if self._grid:            return {param: self._configs[param][i] for i, param in zip(_index[-1], self._grid)}        return self._configs[_index[-1][0]]    def settings(self):        '''returns FedEx input settings'''        output = {'configs': deepcopy(self._configs)}        output['eta0'], output['sched'] = self._eta0, self._sched        output['cutoff'], output['baseline'] = self._cutoff, self._baseline         if self._trace['refine']:            output['theta'] = self.theta()        return output    def step(self):        '''takes exponentiated gradient step (calls 'communication_round' once)'''        index = []        before, after, weight = self._server.communication_round(lambda: self.sample(_index=index))                before, after = np.array(before), np.array(after)        weight = np.array(weight, dtype=np.float64) / sum(weight)        if self._trace['refine']:            trace = self.trace('refine')            if self._diff:                trace -= self.trace('global')            baseline = discounted_mean(trace, self._baseline)        else:            baseline = 0.0        self._trace['global'].append(np.inner(before, weight))        self._trace['refine'].append(np.inner(after, weight))        if not index:            self._trace['entropy'].append(0.0)            self._trace['mle'].append(1.0)            return        for i, (z, theta) in enumerate(zip(self._z, self._theta)):            grad = np.zeros(len(z))            for idx, s, w in zip(index, after-before if self._diff else after, weight):                grad[idx[i]] += w * (s - baseline) / theta[idx[i]]            if self._sched == 'adaptive':                self._store[i] += norm(grad, float('inf')) ** 2                denom = np.sqrt(self._store[i])            elif self._sched == 'aggressive':                denom = 1.0 if np.all(grad == 0.0) else norm(grad, float('inf'))            elif self._sched == 'auto':                self._store[i] += 1.0                denom = np.sqrt(self._store[i])            elif self._sched == 'constant':                denom = 1.0            elif self._sched == 'scale':                denom = 1.0 / np.sqrt(2.0 * np.log(len(grad))) if len(grad) > 1 else float('inf')            else:                raise NotImplementedError            eta = self._eta0[i] / denom            z -= eta * grad            z -= logsumexp(z)            self._theta[i] = np.exp(z)        self._trace['entropy'].append(self.entropy())        self._trace['mle'].append(self.mle())        if self._trace['entropy'][-1] < self._cutoff:            self.stop()    def test(self, mle=False):        '''evaluates found config (calls 'full_evaluation' once)        Args:            mle: use MLE config instead of sampling        Returns:            output of 'full_evaluation'        '''        before, after, weight = self._server.full_evaluation(lambda: self.sample(mle=mle))        return {'global': np.inner(before, weight) / weight.sum(),                'refine': np.inner(after, weight) / weight.sum()}    def theta(self):        '''returns copy of config probability vector'''        return deepcopy(self._theta)    def trace(self, key):        '''returns trace of one of three tracked quantities        Args:            key: 'entropy', 'global', or 'refine'        Returns:            numpy vector with length equal to number of calls to 'step'        '''        return np.array(self._trace[key])class Server:    '''object for federated training implementing methods required by FedEx'''    def _set_test_state(self):        state = (np.random.get_state(), torch.get_rng_state(), torch.cuda.get_rng_state())        if self._state is None:            self._state = state        else:            np.random.set_state(self._state[0])            torch.set_rng_state(self._state[1])            torch.cuda.set_rng_state(self._state[2])        return state    def _reset_state(self, state):        np.random.set_state(state[0])        torch.set_rng_state(state[1])        torch.cuda.set_rng_state(state[2])    def __init__(                 self,                  model,                  clients,                  train,                  test,                  lr=1.0,                  momentum=0.0,                  step=1,                  gamma=1.0,                  batch=10,                 state=None,                 ):        '''        Args:            model: PyTorch model            clients: list of clients, each a function that takes one or more strings 'train',                     'val', 'test' and returns, as one tuple, input and output tensors for each            train: method that takes as argument a PyTorch model, an input tensor, an output                   tensor, and optional kwargs and returns the same PyTorch model            test: method that takes as argument a PyTorch model, an input tensor, and an output                  tensor and returns the model's error            lr: server learning rate            momentum: server momentum            step: server learning rate decay interval            gamma: server learning rate decay factor            batch: number of clients to sample per communication round            state: np.random, torch, torch.cuda random state tuple; if None uses current states        '''        self._model = model        self._clients = clients        self._train = train        self._test = test        self._opt = optim.SGD(self._model.parameters(), lr=lr, momentum=momentum)        self._sched = optim.lr_scheduler.StepLR(self._opt, step, gamma=gamma)        self._batch = batch        self._state = state        self._reset_state(self._set_test_state())    def communication_round(self, get_config):        '''runs one step of local training and model aggregation        Args:            get_config: returns kwargs for 'train' as a dict        Returns:            np.array objects for global val error, local val error, and val size of each client        '''        self._model.cuda()        before, after, weight = [np.zeros(self._batch) for _ in range(3)]        total = 0.0        for i in range(self._batch):            Xtrain, Ytrain, Xval, Yval = random.choice(self._clients)('train', 'val')            before[i] = self._test(self._model, Xval, Yval)            model = self._train(deepcopy(self._model), Xtrain, Ytrain, **get_config())            after[i] = self._test(model, Xval, Yval)            weight[i] = len(Yval)            total += len(Ytrain)            if i:                for agg, param in zip(aggregate.parameters(), model.parameters()):                    agg.data += len(Ytrain) * param.data            else:                for param in model.parameters():                    param.data *= len(Ytrain)                aggregate = model        self._opt.zero_grad()        for agg, param in zip(aggregate.parameters(), self._model.parameters()):            param.grad = param.data - agg / total        self._opt.step()        self._opt.zero_grad()        self._sched.step()        self._model.cpu()        return before, after, weight    def full_evaluation(self, get_config):        '''evaluates personalization on each client        Args:            get_config: returns kwargs for 'train' as a dict        Returns:            np.array objects for global test error, local test error, and test size of each client        '''        state = self._set_test_state()        self._model.cuda()        before, after, weight = [np.zeros(len(self._clients)) for _ in range(3)]        for i, client in enumerate(self._clients):            Xtrain, Ytrain, Xtest, Ytest = client('train', 'test')            before[i] = self._test(self._model, Xtest, Ytest)            after[i] = self._test(self._train(deepcopy(self._model),                                               Xtrain, Ytrain, **get_config()),                                   Xtest, Ytest)            weight[i] = len(Ytest)            print('\r\tEvaluated client', frac(i+1, len(self._clients)),                  '    global error:', round(np.inner(before, weight) / weight.sum(), 4),                  '    refine error:', round(np.inner(after, weight) / weight.sum(), 4),                  end=32*' ')        self._model.cpu()        self._reset_state(state)        return before, after, weightdef random_search(max_resources=500, total_resources=2000):    '''returns a random search rate and schedule for use by 'successive_elimination'    Args:        max_resources: most resources (steps) assigned to single arm        total_resources: overall resource limit    Returns:        elimination rate as an int, elimination schedule as a list    '''    assert max_resources > 0, \"max_resources must be positive\"    return int(total_resources / max_resources), [max_resources]def get_schedule(                 max_resources=500,                  total_resources=2000,                  elim_rate=3,                  num_elim=0,                  num_eval=1,                 ):    '''returns rate and schedule for use by 'successive_elimination'    Args:        max_resources: most resources (steps) assigned to single arm        total_resources: overall resource limit        elim_rate: multiplicative elimination rate        num_elim: number of elimination rounds; if 0 runs random search        num_eval: number of evaluation rounds    Returns:        elimination rate as an int, elimination schedule as a list, evaluation schedule as a list    '''    assert max_resources <= total_resources, \"max_resources cannot be greater than total_resources\"    assert elim_rate > 1, \"elim_rate must be greater than 1\"    assert num_eval <= total_resources, \"num_eval cannot be greater than total_resources\"    if num_elim:        diff = total_resources - max_resources        geos = (elim_rate**(num_elim+1) - 1) / (elim_rate-1)        u = int(diff / (geos-num_elim-1))        resources = 0        v = lambda i: 1 + ceil((diff+(num_elim-geos+elim_rate**i)*u) / (elim_rate**i-1))        for opt in product(*(range(u, v(i)) for i in reversed(range(1, num_elim+1)))):            used = max_resources + sum((elim_rate**i-1)*r                                        for i, r in zip(reversed(range(1, num_elim+1)), opt))            if resources <= used <= total_resources:                best, resources = opt, used        assert not 0 in best, \"invalid: use more resources or fewer eliminations, or increase rate\"        elim_sched = list(np.cumsum(best)) + [max_resources]    else:        elim_rate, elim_sched = random_search(max_resources=max_resources,                                               total_resources=total_resources)    eval_sched = [int(step) for step in np.linspace(0, total_resources, num_eval+1)[1:]]    return elim_rate, elim_sched, eval_scheddef successive_elimination(                           sampler,                            eval_traces,                            logdir=None,                            val_discount=0.0,                            elim_rate=3,                            elim_sched=[1],      ):    '''runs successive elimination according to provided schedule    Args:        sampler: function of n returning an iterable of n objects with methods 'step' and 'trace'        eval_traces:        kwargs: passed to 'test' method of each config    Returns:        best config;        also dumps tensorboard logs and results.pkl to folder 'logdir', if specified    '''    assert   traces, eval_sched = deepcopy(traces), deepcopy(eval_sched)    for trace in eval_traces:        if not trace in traces:            traces.append(trace)    #each config is (index, config_settings())    configs = list(enumerate(sampler(elim_rate ** max(1, len(elim_sched)-1))))    output = {index: {'settings': config.settings()} for index, config in configs}    for trace in eval_traces:        output[trace+' val'] = []        output[trace+' test'] = []    output['eval step'] = []    changed = {index: True for index, _ in configs}    #evaluate fedex object with best score across all time    best_score = 100.0    best_config = None     best_config_idx = None    start, last_start, used = 0, 0, 0    for i, stop in enumerate(elim_sched):        if len(configs) == 1 and not last_round is None:            getattr(configs[0][1], last_round)()        scores = []        for j, (index, config) in enumerate(configs):            scores.append(float('inf'))            for k in range(start, stop):                changed[index] = True                print('\r\tRound', frac(i+1, len(elim_sched)),                       'config', frac(j+1, len(configs)),                       'step', frac(k+1, stop), end=4*' ')                config.step()                for trace in traces:                    output[index][trace] = config.trace(trace)                    print(trace+':', round(output[index][trace][-1], 4), end=4*' ')                    if logger:                        logger.add_scalars(trace, {str(index): output[index][trace][-1]}, k+1)                #use refine error, if eval_global use global error                if eval_global:                    scores[-1] = discounted_mean(output[index][eval_traces[1]][start:],val_discount)                else:                    scores[-1] = discounted_mean(output[index][eval_traces[0]][start:], val_discount)                used += 1                current_best, score = min(enumerate(scores), key=itemgetter(1))                #check if val score beats best score so far                if score < best_score:                     best_config_idx = configs[current_best][0]                    best_config = deepcopy(configs[current_best][1])                    best_score = score                 print('best:', round(best_score, 4), end=8*' ')                for trace in eval_traces:                    if len(output[best_config_idx][trace][start:])==0:                        val = discounted_mean(output[best_config_idx][trace][last_start:], val_discount)                    else:                        val = discounted_mean(output[best_config_idx][trace][start:], val_discount)                    output[trace+' val'].append(val)                    if logger:                        logger.add_scalar(trace+' val', val, used)                if used in eval_sched:                    if changed[best_config_idx]:                        results = best_config.test(**kwargs)                        changed[best_config_idx] = False                    print('\r\tStep', used, 'test error', end='')                    for trace in reversed(eval_traces): pickle.dump(output, f)        try:            logger.flush()        except AttributeError:            pass    return configdef wrapped_fedex(                  get_server,                  get_client,                  num_configs=1,                  prod=False,                  stepsize_init='auto',                   stepsize_sched='aggressive',                   cutoff=1E-4,                  baseline_discount=-1.0,                   diff=False,                  mle=False,                  logdir=None,                  val_discount=0.0,                   last_stop=False,                  eval_global=False,                  **kwargs,                  ):    '''evaluates FedEx wrapped with successive elimination algorithm;       uses FedAvg when num_configs = 1 and prod = False    Args:        get_server: function that takes no input and returns an object that can be passed as the                     first argument to FedEx.__init__, e.g. a Server object        get_client: function directly                     - =0: value drawn at random from Unif[1, number of arms given by the wrapper]                     - =-1: use the number of arms given by the wrapper                     - -baseline_discount)    else:        baseline = lambda: baseline_discount    def sampler(n):        for _ in range(n):            yield FedEx(                        get_server(),     "Experiment Result": "The FedEx method is implemented with a wrapper algorithm, which can be random search or successive elimination. The hyperparameter search space for the server includes learning rate (lr) from 10^-1 to 10^1, momentum (0.0 or 0.9), and learning rate decay parameters (step and gamma from 10^-4 to 10^-2). For clients, the search space includes learning rate (lr) from 10^-4 to 10^0, momentum from 0.0 to 1.0, weight decay from 10^-5 to 10^-1, epochs (1 to 5), batch size (2^3 to 2^7), mu for FedProx (10^-5 to 10^0), and dropout from 0.0 to 0.5.A local perturbation scheme is used to initialize discrete configurations for the categorical distribution over hyperparameters. This involves starting with an initial client configuration and generating `n_clients-1` additional configurations by perturbing each hyperparameter: lr, momentum, weight_decay, epochs, batch, mu, and dropout with a multiplicative perturbation factor `eps`. For example, log_lr is perturbed by `np.random.uniform(4*-eps, 4*eps)`.The `wrapped_fedex` function integrates FedEx with wrapper algorithms. Key FedEx-specific settings include: `--configs` (number of configs to optimize over), `--eps` (multiplicative perturbation to client config, 0.0 for FedAvg), `--uniform`/`--random` (to run FedEx over uniform/random product grids), `--eta0` (FedEx initial step size, 'auto' by default), `--sched` (step size schedule for exponentiated gradient, 'aggressive' by default), `--cutoff` (entropy level to stop updating config distribution), `--baseline` (discount factor for computing baseline, -1.0 means sampling from [0.0, abs(baseline)]), `--diff` (use performance difference as FedEx objective), `--stop` (stop updating distribution after last elimination), `--mle` (use MLE config at test time), and `--eval_global` (use global error as elimination metric).The wrapper algorithm settings include: `--rounds` (max communication rounds, default 800 for Cifar, 50 for Femnist, 800 for Shakespeare), `--total` (total communication rounds, default 4000 for Cifar/Shakespeare, 450 for Femnist), `--rate` (elimination rate, default 3), `--elim` (number of elimination rounds, default 0), `--eval` (number of evaluation rounds, default 1), and `--discount` (discount factor for validation score, default 0.0).The experiments are conducted on CIFAR-10 using a CNN model, FEMNIST using a CNN model, and Shakespeare using a CharLSTM model. Data settings include `--val` proportion (0.2 for Cifar, 0.1 for Femnist/Shakespeare), `--num-clients` (500 for Cifar), `--iid` (for Femnist/Shakespeare), `--test` proportion (0.1 for Femnist/Shakespeare), `--length` for Shakespeare (80 chars), `--hidden` (256) and `--layers` (2) for CharLSTM."
